FR1's 28 among them). No map was needed: `observationMetadata` was complete since FR1, and VIREO's E03 names were among FR1's
originals. The one map string E03 can show, `spectralRange.locked` (Spectral Range… disabled by the instructor's Allow User
Change), belongs to the `spectralRange` map, which FR2 translates with the spectrometer.
- **Identical to English** (`identical.json`, PT3's reasons): the placeholder-only formats `classLines.option`, `photo.caption`,
  `viewport.range`, `nativeResults.typeValue`, `nativeResults.kindValue`, `program.row`, `nativeText.choice`, the hash name
  `probe.shaLabel`, and the Delphi type names «Single · 32 bits» and «Double · 64 bits» («Extended · 80 bits stockés» is
  translated, as in Spanish and Portuguese).
- **Shared with FR2:** `continuity.restoreExposure` «Restaurer cette acquisition dans le télescope», `ssp.header` «Objet : {object}
  · pointage AD {ra} h / Déc. {dec}° (époque non précisée)» and `classLink.open` «Classer l’étoile acquise» are byte-identical in
  both worktrees (checked against `claude/front-fr2` before the hand-back).

**How it was translated.** Five Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`), from the English with the reviewed Spanish and Portuguese
as meaning context only, under one brief (FR0/FR1's: vous, infinitive controls, vous-imperative instructions, sentence case,
ordinary spaces before ; : ! ? and inside « » for the fixer, ’, the IAU OAE terms with «raie» never «ligne» for a spectral line,
the glossary's VIREO names with the English at the first mention in prose, and FR1's E03 control names that its guide keys quote):
the lab, guide, line table, import and combined view (167 keys); the DAT edit/delete/list and TXT/CSV inspectors (162); the
notebook and the shared native-results texts (179); the width measurement, cursor, comparison marks and Program List (117); the
sampled flux, photo, viewport, inspection, trace, difference and range displays (118). Merged with `scripts/i18n-batch.mjs merge
--lang fr`, then the fixer's fix mode, then the strict lint. The Opus implementer read every row against the English and corrected
63 strings (8.5 %):
- **One form each:** recovered (reverse-engineered) › «récupéré» everywhere, as FR1 writes it (the displays batch had «retrouvé»,
  10 strings); policy › «règle» (4 strings had «politique»); a DAT or TXT time field › «instant», as VIREO's «Instant de
  l’observation (JD)» (8 strings had «temps»); Review … for a dialog › «Vérifier …», as `class.review` (4 notebook strings had
  «Examiner»); Reverse trace displays › «Inverser l’affichage des tracés», as FR1's guide and `classLab.step.inspect.reverse` name
  it (2); Set classification wavelength range › «Définir la plage de longueurs d’onde de la classification», as the guide names it
  (3); round half to even › «arrondies à l’entier le plus proche (pair en cas d’égalité)» in both policies; the Shift key «Maj»
  (two had «Maj.»); «exportation» for the noun (two had «export»); «Objet d’entraînement d’origine» for the width source kind and
  «Charger l’objet d’entraînement sélectionné»; the Delphi type names kept (above).
- **Meaning:** `classLink.noDifference` "the recorded Difference mode" › «Le mode Différence, tel qu’il a été relevé» (VIREO's mode
  as the research recorded it; the batch had «consigné», which reads as a student's record); the two "Read …" policies describe what
  is read («Sont lus…», «Les textes déclarés fournis sont lus…», were «Lit…»); `class.notGraded` «consignée» (Record › Consigner);
  `classImport.backup` "Practice-only saves" › «Un fichier enregistré limité à l’entraînement» («sauvegarde» is backup there);
  "byte offsets" › «positions d’octets» (never a bare «décalage», plan §5.1); "hashed" › «son empreinte n’a pas pu être calculée»
  (was «haché»); "byte ownership was lost" › «si les octets ne sont plus conservés»; "Native observation time word" › «Valeur
  native de l’instant d’observation»; «Texte déclaré d’origine conservé»; a colon-only type «formée uniquement de deux-points»;
  "workflow" › «démarche» (was «flux», the flux of a spectrum); «graphique de différence» for the plot (tracé is a trace);
  «Marquage des panneaux de comparaison»; «minus» as «moins» in the two difference titles; «Entrées de classification · {count}».
- **Typography and lint:** «distincte de l’option Inverser…» (elision before a control name), «à l’intérieur de 3500–7400 Å» (the
  glyph count), «144 000 octets» (FR1's thousands rule for five digits and more; «1000» as the English writes it, everywhere).

**Glossary.** Terms tightened: snapshot › «instantané» (was instantané|capture), pane › «panneau» (was panneau|volet). Added:
practice › «entraînement», draft › «brouillon», fallback › «repli». Other choices in one form: standard › étalon, standard pair ›
paire d’étalons, practice object › objet d’entraînement, record (a lab record) › entrée, record (a DAT record) › enregistrement,
Record › consigner, trace › tracé, plot › graphique, readout › lecture, combined view › vue combinée, bins › canaux, bracket › jeu
de marques, marker › repère, shoulder › épaule, owned › conservé, hash/fingerprint › empreinte, payload › charge utile, runtime ›
environnement d’exécution, native › natif, Undo (noun) › annulation, recovery copy › copie de récupération, notebook › carnet
d’observation, zoom in/out › agrandir/réduire, pan › déplacer la vue, Classify (VIREO) › Classer (Classify).

**Tests.**
- `tests/i18n/languageText.ts`: `FRENCH_TEXT.done` gains `e03`, so `e03.spec.ts` runs its five checks in French under their own
  titles (the walk's English and French texts, View original, the French originals, 320 and 1440 px). No `SAME_IN.fr` was needed:
  no French text of the walk equals the English outside the shared identifiers. The star exposure buttons the walk passes through
  are E01's (FR2): `textIn` gives the preview's English for a `starExposure.` key until `FRENCH_TEXT.done` has `e01`, and throws for
  any other key French lacks (FR3 review).
- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: the E03 family (session, classification CSV, investigations, notebook and edit recovery,
  DAT unchanged and edited, TXT, the generated native CSV and the imported spectrum) gains `[fr]`: same names and SHA-256 as English,
  no French letter, « » or U+202F in any file, and the flow's `h1` is the French title. Its three star exposure buttons are matched
  by their English while `FRENCH_TEXT.done` lacks `e01`, then through `N()` like every E03 control, which fails for a name French
  lacks (FR3 review: the `text` lookup would have let an untranslated label pass after FR2).
- `tests/i18n/areas.spec.ts`: the pinned French areas are now `classification`, `cluster`, `fits`, `photometer` (the cluster routes
  fetch the shared `classification+cluster` module); pulsar, radio, lss and exposure stay empty.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`; compared with main 2eb0c25 built the same way, chunk hashes normalised). fr: 6
chunks, 99.2 KB gzip (66.8 KB after FR1) — core 65.8 KB gzip (216.1 KB raw; 63.3 KB after FR1), classification 29.8 KB gzip
(112.1 KB raw; Portuguese's is 28.1, Spanish's 28.0), classification+cluster 0.4, cluster 0.2 (no key of its own: found by its name
only, with the build's warning, as pt-BR's after PT3), photometer 1.8, fits 1.2; pulsar, radio, LSS and exposure still empty.
Spanish and Portuguese: every chunk byte-identical, names included. English: every file identical once chunk hashes are normalised
(the entry included: the fr core imports no new module); the two same-named shared chunk pairs (`clusterComparison`,
`clusterSelection`) only swap their hash order. Deploy limits: 2,172 files.

**Verification** (2026-11-11, worktree `claude/front-fr3` from main 2eb0c25). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`,
`src/seo`, `src/components` and `src/core/classificationProgram.test.ts`: 1,111 tests in 45 files (the IME audit's pin scan timed
out once at 5 s while a second build ran, and passed alone: a load flake); the pins refreshed with no change; `npm run build` with
every check. Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig, two workers): `areas`, `observatory`, `shell` and `e03` 99 (three
failed in that run, which shared the machine with `tsc` and Vitest: the Spanish E03 originals test, the French observatory walk and
the qps observatory walk; all three passed when rerun alone, load flakes); the five French `e03` tests alone; the exports-identity
`[fr]` variants (E03, hot list, options file, Field Information, toolbar) and the E03 family in es-419 and pt-BR, 7; `npm run
test:scripts` 173; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/` 77. The fr 320 px screenshots of the guide, the width review and the imported
spectrum's Observational Data were inspected.

**Independent review (FR3).** Every one of the 743 strings was read against the English. Seven strings changed (six keys of this
front and the FR1 guide key that quotes one of them):
1. *Translations (low; fixed).* `program.choose` «Entraînement selon la liste du programme» named a select of practice objects as an
   activity › «Objet d’entraînement de la liste du programme», and FR1's `classLab.step.choose.help`, which quotes it, follows;
   `photo.plotPolicy` had lost the trace ("A labelled trace fallback") › «Un tracé de repli étiqueté conserve…»;
   `combined.regionPolicy` "a separate reviewed action" had become «que vous contrôlez vous-même» › «une action distincte, avec sa
   propre vérification»; `obs.file.conflict` called the restore review «examen» where its button says «Vérifier la restauration…» ›
   «vérification»; `nativeList.append` «Ajouter à la fin des enregistrements…» read as "add at the end of the records" › «Ajouter
   des enregistrements DAT depuis un fichier»; `nativeCsv.projection` «Le remplissage de zéros» › «Les zéros de remplissage».
2. *The star exposure fallback (medium; tightened).* The export family's `text()` lookup and the walk's `textIn` fell back to the
   English for any key French lacked, so after FR2 an untranslated star exposure label, or any E03 key the walk names, would have
   matched the page's English and passed. Now only a `starExposure.` key may fall back, and only until `FRENCH_TEXT.done` has
   `e01`; after that the export family goes through `N()` and the walk's `textIn` throws, as for a released language.
3. *The keyless cluster chunk (accepted).* Its warning is pt-BR's after PT3: `i18n-fr-cluster` only re-exports the shared
   `classification+cluster` module (no key of its own, so no marker to find it by). The check makes that an offender for a released
   language, so French cannot be released before FR4 gives `cluster` (E04's cluster tools) its own text, which it must do anyway.

**Left for later fronts.** FR2: E01 and E02, the star exposure buttons the E03 walk and export family pass through, and the
`spectralRange` map (its `locked` note shows in E03 only when the instructor turns off Allow User Change). FR4–FR6 and FR-HELP: their
areas, and the classification help files behind the lab's Help button (CLASSHLP, SPECLNS) and the Spectral Line Table window's own
text. FR-REL: the partial-language areas case back to a released-language test.

## RTL groundwork: RTL lint profiles and direction plumbing (RT8, RT9)

Fronts RT8 and RT9 (plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-rtl.md` § 3.2–3.4, § 4, § 7 and their rows of § 9), on one branch. Groundwork
only: no Arabic, Hebrew or Persian catalog is added, none is registered, and **nothing changes for a left-to-right visitor** (proof
below). The user decided on 2026-11-11 that Arabic, if it is ever translated, addresses the student in the singular, neutral register;
there is no Hebrew or Persian for now, so their profiles are minimal checks.

### RT8: the right-to-left lint profiles

`src/i18n/lint/profiles.ts` gains `ar` (complete), `he` and `fa` (minimal), with `REGISTER` `ar:'singular'`, `he:'plural'` and
`fa:'shoma'`. Each rule has good and bad mutation fixtures in `fixtures.ts` (ar 38, he 25, fa 26), run by `rules.test.ts` as for
every profile, including a flipped register for each language; `src/i18n/lint/rtl.test.ts` adds what the fixtures cannot show.
- **Digits** (`rules.ts` `digits`, every language): native digits were already refused (٠–٩ and ۰–۹ are `\p{Nd}`); the Arabic
  decimal and thousands separators ٫ and ٬ are now refused too. No existing catalog holds them (the es, pt-BR and fr lints pass
  unchanged).
- **Punctuation** (ar, fa): an ASCII `,` `;` `?` after Arabic-script text, or before it (`{file}, ثم`), must be ، ؛ ؟; `?` and ؟
  count as one glyph class, and `?lang=` is no question mark. Hebrew writes Latin punctuation and refuses ، ؛ ؟ (its `quotes`
  rule). Quotes: Arabic « » or “ ”, Persian « », never `"`.
- **Glyph classes** (all three): → and ←, ▸ and ◂, ↗ and ↖ are one class each, so a translation may point an arrow along its
  reading; › ⟦ brackets and « » mirror by themselves. The en dash is not counted, because the range rule asks for words.
- **Letter purity**: Arabic refuses Persian ی and ک (use ي and ك), Persian refuses Arabic ي, ك and ى (use ی and ک), through the
  existing `otherScript` mechanism. Hebrew presentation forms (U+FB1D–FB4F), Arabic ones (U+FB50–FDFF and U+FE70–FEFE, except the
  ornate parentheses ﴾ ﴿ and the rial sign ﷼, which are punctuation and a currency sign) and tatweel (U+0640) are refused.
- **Joiners**: `LocaleProfile.joiners` (new, optional) narrows `joinerScript` to the non-joiner: Persian allows ZWNJ only between two
  Arabic-script characters (می‌کنید, پرونده‌ها), never ZWJ, never before a space; Arabic and Hebrew have no joiner script, so any
  joiner fails as before.
- **Order rules** (all three; RT7's decision that catalog literals are the lint's, `tests/fixtures/rtlKnown.ts` `catalogLiteral` and
  `signBeforeParam`): `{x}–{y}` and `{x}-{y}` (also between map samples «A1»; an em dash between two values is a separator and
  passes, since «{b} — {a}» reads right to left as «{a} — {b}»), which a right-to-left sentence draws «y–x», must be
  words (من {a} إلى {b}, מ־{a} עד {b}, از {a} تا {b}); a sign before a value (`−{threshold}`) belongs in the value; a signed
  number (`−21.4`, `+54`) or a hyphenated date (`2009-06-01`, `11-24-2008`) typed into the text becomes a `{param}`, which `t()`
  isolates; an ellipsis after a Latin word (`ID…`, `(Save As...)`) is drawn before it, so the translation ends the Latin text without
  it. Unsigned ranges of numbers (`3700–4700 Å`), versions, times, codes such as `0332+5434` and format tokens (`YYYY-MM-DD`) pass
  (RT7 found them read correctly). Copied literally into a right-to-left catalog, en.json's 3,594 strings would trip 40 signed
  numbers, 13 placeholder ranges and 3 dates: the first right-to-left batch rewrites them.
- **Register**: Arabic singular and neutral refuses the plural pronouns (أنتم، أنتن، أنتما), the plural imperatives of the
  interface's verbs (اضغطوا، اختاروا…), feminine-only imperatives (اضغطي…) and gender slashes (a slash before a feminine or plural ending: اختر/ي، الطالب/ة،
  المعلمين/ات; «و/أو» and units such as كم/ث pass), with و or ف attached too, except و before ض (وضع، وضعي and وضعوا are words of
  their own); «plural» stays expressible. Hebrew's proposal is the gender-neutral plural imperative (לחצו, בחרו; refuses
  אתה and the singular imperatives, with ו attached, leaving out those that are also everyday words unpointed: לחץ, שמור, סגור,
  בדוק); Persian's is «شما» (refuses تو and the informal imperatives کن، بزن…). Both
  are proposals of the plan, not decisions, since neither language is planned.
- **Claims**: ترجمة رسمية, الترجمة الرسمية or ترجمة CLEA (never ليست…, غير رسمية); תרגום רשמי, התרגום הרשמי or תרגום של CLEA
  (never לא/אינו); ترجمه رسمی, ترجمهٔ or ترجمۀ رسمی, or ترجمه CLEA (never غیررسمی or … نیست).
- **Plurals**: Arabic requires all six CLDR forms (zero, one, two, few, many, other; none optional), Hebrew one/two/other and
  Persian one/other, each equal to `Intl.PluralRules` (`pluralForms` already enforces any set).
- **Length bands** are first proposals for AR0 to calibrate: ar 0.6–1.4, he 0.5–1.3, fa 0.7–1.5 (grapheme clusters).
- **`iso()` in map functions** (`src/i18n/lint/mapIsolates.ts`, test support on `sourceScan.ts`): inside any function of a map file,
  every `${…}` that is not `iso(…)`, every operand of a string concatenation (`'…'+x`, `iso(a)+x`) that is not text or `iso(…)`,
  and every value substituted by `.replace`, `.replaceAll` or `.concat` that is not text or `iso(…)` is reported as
  `place · expression` (text inside an `iso(…)` argument is one isolated value, but it may hold no right-to-left letter:
  `iso(\`${n} نجمة\`)` isolates the sentence, not the value). `iso` must be `bidi.ts`'s, imported under its own name; a local or
  other `iso` is reported. `rtl.test.ts` runs it over `src/i18n/<dir>/maps/` of
  every registered language whose `direction` is `rtl` (none today, so the first one is checked with no change to the test), passes
  and fails synthetic map sources, and shows on Spanish's `objectXLab.ts` that a map written the left-to-right way is caught. RT4's
  `iso()` in `src/i18n/bidi.ts` is reused; no new helper module.

### RT9: direction plumbing

Everything follows the registry's `direction` (RT1), so it works when a right-to-left language is added; for a left-to-right
language in a left-to-right page there is no attribute and no isolate.
- `src/i18n/languages.ts` `directionWithin(tag,page,registry)`: a language's own direction where it differs from the page's, else
  undefined (React then writes no attribute). `src/i18n/isolates.ts` gains `RLI` (U+2067) and `directionalIsolate(text,dir)`.
- **Selector** (`src/components/LanguageChoice.tsx`): each `<option>` gets `dir` beside `lang` when its language runs the other way
  (an Arabic option in an English page, English and the other left-to-right options in an Arabic page); the reload dialog's sentence
  isolates the language's name (`dialogLanguageName`: RLI or LRI…PDI). `LanguageSelect` takes an optional test-only `registry`.
- **Failure notices** (`src/OpenVireo.tsx`): each half gets `dir` when it differs from the page: the right-to-left language's own
  sentence in the English page its failed chunk leaves, or English inside a right-to-left page whose area failed.
- **SEO** (`src/seo/languages.ts` `seoDirection`, `src/seo/pages.ts`): a right-to-left language's pages get `<html lang dir="rtl">`;
  each language link gets `dir` when it differs from its page. The page builders and `languageLinks` take an optional registry
  for the tests. `scripts/render-og-image.mjs` writes `dir="rtl"` for such a language and places its blocks with
  `inset-inline-start` instead of `left`, so the preview image mirrors (checked once by forcing `rtl` in a scratch copy).
- **Tests**: `src/components/languageDirection.test.ts` (5: `directionWithin`, `dialogLanguageName`, the selector's markup with no
  `dir` today, `dir="rtl"` on released Arabic, Hebrew and Persian options of the test registry, `dir="ltr"` on every other option
  in an Arabic page) and `src/seo/direction.test.ts` (4: every row's direction is the registry's; no page has `dir` today; with a
  test registry marking Spanish right to left, its pages have `<html lang="es-419" dir="rtl">` and links `dir="ltr"`, and only the
  Spanish link of an English or Portuguese page has `dir="rtl"`).

### LTR identity

- **`dist/`**: main b519257 and this branch built the same way (`npm run build`), every asset name's chunk hash normalised and
  same-named chunks paired by content: the same 2,171 files; **every HTML page (index.html, `/labs/`, `/es/`, `/pt-br/`), the
  sitemap, every CSS file and every non-JavaScript file is byte-identical**. 2,138 files are identical after normalisation; of the
  33 others, 30 differ only in the minifier's names for the shared chunk's exports, which shift with its three new exports (same
  size; checked by comparing each pair with its import and export lists set aside, where only `bidi` and `screenDate` differ, by
  their local names), and three hold the new code: the
  shared `jsx-runtime` chunk +106 B (`directionWithin`, `RLI`, `directionalIsolate`), the entry +39 B (the notices' `dir`) and
  `LanguageChoice` +161 B.
- **Screen**: both `dist/` served on loopback (vite preview) and captured in Chromium: the selector's `outerHTML`, the reload dialog
  for every other option and `<html lang dir>` in en-US, es-MX and pt-BR browsers and on `?lang=fr`, and both failure notices
  (core chunk refused, `classification` area refused) for es-419, pt-BR and fr: 23 captures, identical.
- **Preview images**: main's and this branch's `render-og-image.mjs` render es-419, pt-BR and en to byte-identical PNGs, equal to
  the committed `public/og-image-es.png` and `og-image-pt-br.png`.

### RT7 `KNOWN` entries

None is removed: neither RT8 nor RT9 changes what the `qps-rtl` walks render. `catalogLiteral` comes from English catalog literals
that `qps-rtl` keeps (changing en.json would change every translation), and `signBeforeParam` needs the sign moved into the radio
antenna maps' value in English, Spanish, Portuguese and French and `iso()` at the message translators' substitution points (RT12).
Their reasons in `tests/fixtures/rtlKnown.ts` now say that the ar, he and fa lints flag both in a real translation. No entry is
owned by RT9.

**Verification** (2026-11-11, worktree `claude/front-rt8` from main b519257, two workers). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest `src/i18n`, `src/seo`,
`src/theme` and `src/components`: 1,092 tests in 40 files (the lint's `rules.test.ts` with the 89 new fixtures and `rtl.test.ts` 8,
`languageDirection.test.ts` 5, `direction.test.ts` 4); the pin refresh (`plural`, `imeAudit`, `split`) leaves every pin unchanged;
`npm run build` with every check. Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig): `tests/i18n/shell.spec.ts`,
`tests/page-bar.spec.ts` and `tests/i18n/language-selector.spec.ts` 59 of 59; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/` 77 of 77; the scripts
job's three parts exactly as the workflow selects them, 107 (6.2 min), 66 (8.4 min) and 17 (3.5 min), all passed, so every RT7
`KNOWN` entry is still met. No failure, so no flake to report.

**Deferred.** AR0 calibrates the length bands, register word lists and glossary; Hebrew and Persian stay minimal until (if ever)
planned; RT12 moves the B1950 sign into its value and isolates the translators' substitutions; an Arabic SEO row (AR-SEO) will be the
first real use of the pages' `dir`, and makes `public/labs/labs.css` logical (its `ol,ul{padding-left:1.4em}` puts the list indent on
the wrong side of a right-to-left page; changing it now would change a deployed file for nothing).

### Review (RT8, RT9)

Independent review on the same branch. **Lint** (realistic singular-neutral Arabic UI text run through the profiles): the gender-slash
pattern read any one- or two-letter word after a slash, so «كم/ث» (km/s, the Hubble lab's unit) and «كم/ث/ميغا فرسخ» failed; it now
reads only the feminine and plural endings. The attached و turned وضعي and وضعوا into feminine and plural imperatives (and, under the
plural setting, وضع «mode» into a singular one); و is no longer read before ض. Hebrew's singular list held לחץ (pressure), שמור
(saved), סגור (closed) and בדוק (checked), everyday words unpointed; they are left out. The Hebrew claim missed «התרגום הרשמי» (the
article) and Persian's missed the precomposed ezafe «ترجمۀ»; both are read now, and «לא התרגום הרשמי» passes. The range rule no longer
reads an em dash separator; ﴾ ﴿ and ﷼ are no presentation forms. **`mapIsolates`** was met by wrappers that isolate nothing: `iso()`
around the whole sentence (`iso(\`${n} نجمة\`)`, which isolates the Arabic with the number and draws «-12.5» as «12.5-»),
`iso(a)+n`, `.replace('{n}',String(n))`, and a local `const iso=x=>x`; each is reported now (`rtl.test.ts`, 11 tests, the three new
ones failing on the first version). Plural categories match `Intl.PluralRules` (ar six, he one/two/other, fa one/other); ی/ک and
ي/ك/ى are the right letter pairs; ZWNJ inside Persian words only is right. **LTR**: main b519257 and the branch head built with
`vite build`, chunk hashes normalised and same-named chunks paired by content: every HTML page, CSS file, the sitemap and every
binary identical; 30 chunks differ only in minifier aliases (token-compared) and three hold the new code. No en, es-419, pt-BR or fr
string holds ٫ or ٬, so the new `digits` refusal cannot fire on them; `inset-inline-start` with no `dir` is `left`. **Hypothetical
right-to-left language** (an uncommitted `direction:"rtl"` on `fr`, then on `es-419`): on `?lang=fr` the page is `<html dir="rtl">`,
the en, es-419 and pt-BR options carry `dir="ltr"` and fr none, the reload dialog reads «…en English, …» with English inside LRI…PDI; a failed core
chunk leaves `lang="en"` with the French half `dir="rtl"`, a failed area leaves the English half `dir="ltr"`. With Spanish marked
right to left, `vite build` wrote `/es/` pages `<html lang="es-419" dir="rtl">` with en and pt-BR links `dir="ltr"`, and English and
Portuguese pages' Spanish links `dir="rtl"`; hreflang alternates are unchanged; the preview image mirrored (brand, title and lines at
the right).

## French: E04 Pleiades and E05 variable stars (FR4)

The fifth French front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-fr.md` §6.2 "FR4" in the research root, with PT4 as its model; it ran
beside FR5 (radio telescope). French stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=fr` reaches it, and fr and fr-* browsers still
open English. The translations are web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's; every file the site writes stays English. VIREO's own
captions in these screens (Record/Review, Observational Data, Display/Print Data, Possible Cluster Member, Select Cluster Data…,
Clusters for Study, Load/Plot, View/Print Parameters, Variable Star Data, Simulated Light Curve, the Cluster Data dialog) were French
since FR1's originals, so this front adds web text only.

**What is translated.**
- **Catalog:** PT4's 585 keys (about 8,600 English words): every key of the prefixes `ph` (the photometer route), `pa` (the Pleiades
  colour–magnitude analysis), `pleiadesLab` (the rest of the Pleiades guide), `nc` (the native cluster model fit), `db`, `ce` and
  `clusterLab` (the cluster reference database, its exclusions and guide), `ownHr` and `obsHr` (Data from Observations and the
  saved-observation HR tool); with FR1's 64 keys of those prefixes, the 649 keys are complete (`fr.test.ts` pins them, `FR4_KEYS`).
  Every E04 and E05 file is at 100 % (`node scripts/i18n-coverage.mjs --lang fr`); `fr.json` now holds 2,815 keys.
- **Maps, complete:** `clusterHr` and `clusterData` (new, `src/i18n/fr/maps/`), and `variableStars` (partial since FR0, only `nav`),
  with `plural('fr',…)` where English has it and the variable-star counts grouped with U+202F («31 147», the registry's `thousands`,
  written as the character, as the fixer writes it).
- **Identical to English** (`identical.json`, 24 rows with reasons): band letters and symbols (`obsHr.blockedField.*`, `obsHr.ebv`,
  `obsHr.modulus`, `obsHr.colBV`, `obsHr.colIndex`, `nc.axisColor`, `db.columnEbv`), placeholder formats (`ph.fixed`,
  `ph.seriesSecondsOption`, `variableStars.typeOption`), SHA-256, «Distance (pc)», «Pixel (x, y)», «magnitudes», the variable-star
  columns Type, Max and «Min / ampl.», the empty action header, the em dash for no period, Portrait (twice) and the help viewer's OK.
- **Lint allowances** (`lint-allowed.json`, 8 rows): the placeholder `{cluster}` in six strings (the forbidden-word rule reads the
  placeholder's name as the English «cluster»; the text says «amas»), the file name CLUSTERS.DAT, and the multiplication dot of c·Z.

**Turn-off › «point de retournement de la séquence principale» (glossary decision, before the batches).** The plan suggested «point
de coude (turn-off)» as uncertain. No French source for «point de coude» was found. French Wikipedia's *Séquence principale*
(revision 240749289, 2026-10-26, fetched 2026-11-11) names the place where a cluster's stars leave the main sequence «le point de
retournement», and dates the cluster from it; Françoise Combes's Collège de France course (2023-2024, *Étoiles et trous noirs*,
lecture of 18 December, fetched 2026-11-11) keeps the English «turn-off» («point de turn-off», «Age des amas d’étoiles, et
turn-off»); the French OpenStax *Astronomie* (LibreTexts) writes «arrêt de la séquence principale». So the descriptive form is
«point de retournement de la séquence principale», with «(turn-off)» at its first mention in a string, the English French
astronomers also use; «coude» and «arrêt» are not used. The glossary row `turn-?off` now requires «point de retournement» (FR0's
loose list «point de coude|coude|point de sortie|point de virage|turn-off» is gone); its three uses are `nc.limits`,
`clusterLab.step.trial.help` and the Cluster Data map's `editModeColumns`. Two term rows were added: membership › «appartenance»
(«appartenance à l’amas», in every string; the batches had no other form), dataset › «jeu(x) de données».

**How it was translated.** Five Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`) for the catalog (113 and 112 `ph` keys; `pa`, `pleiadesLab`
and `nc`, 107; `db`, `ce` and `clusterLab`, 127; `ownHr` and `obsHr`, 126) and one for the three maps, from the English with the
reviewed Spanish and Portuguese beside each row as meaning context only, under one brief (FR1–FR3's: vous, infinitive controls,
vous-imperative instructions, sentence case, ordinary spaces before ; : ! ? and inside « » for the fixer, ’, the IAU OAE terms, the
glossary's and `originals.json`'s VIREO names with VIREO's English at a first mention in prose, the turn-off decision above, and the
French already on screen for these areas: FR1's 64 guide and photometer keys). Merged with `scripts/i18n-batch.mjs merge --lang fr`,
then the fixer's fix mode, then the strict lint. The Opus implementer read every row against the English and changed 80 catalog
strings (13.7 %) and 13 map strings beyond typography:
- **One form each:** "Inspect" › «Inspecter» (three strings wrote «Examiner»; FR2's and FR3's controls say «Inspecter»); "Retry
  selected model" › «Réessayer de charger le modèle sélectionné» on both buttons (`nc.retry`, `obsHr.retryModel`); «Confirmer
  l’ajustement du modèle choisi» and its two citations; "Full investigation saves" › «Un enregistrement de l’investigation complète»
  (three); "Hotlist {n}" › «Entrée {n} de la liste d’objets» (one wrote «Étoile»); «Confirmé d’après …» for the three fit sources;
  «(avec défilement)» for the three scrollable regions; "route" › «voie» (FR1's word; two wrote «parcours»); select placeholders in
  the infinitive («Choisir un jeu de données», «Choisir un point de référence»); «remarque» for the notebook note; «Notez vos
  lectures à la main» (the batch wrote «Consignez», VIREO's Record, for the web notebook); the Pleiades guide «guide du TP
  Pléiades», as FR0's `pleiadesLab.nav`.
- **VIREO names and glosses:** English glosses holding «Cluster» (Select Cluster Data, Possible Cluster Member, Cluster Data, Cluster
  Data Parameters, CLEA Cluster Data, Cluster Age/Distance Parameters) are dropped, since the French name already stands (FR2's
  treatment of «Redshift»); Test Mode's report line «Distance estimée de l’amas», as FR1's `testMode.adaptation` quotes it; the Tools
  menu items with VIREO's English in brackets, as Spanish and Portuguese (`obsHr.zams`, `obsHr.isochrone`, `obsHr.clear`); options
  agree with «l’option … activée/désactivée» (Time-Dependent Data, Show Object ID, Detect Sky); «paramètres du photomètre définis par
  l’enseignant» (was «du mode enseignant»); the manual's title «Photométrie photoélectrique des Pléiades» with its English gloss.
- **Meaning and grammar:** VIREO's telescope names already say Telescope («0.4m (16") Telescope»), so «le {telescope}» never «le
  télescope {telescope}» (four strings, two of them FR1's `ph.domainLimit.*`; the review added FR1's two `ph.tiledLimit.*`); «Source du rougissement non appliqué / confirmé» (the
  participle belongs to the reddening, PT4's finding); a reading is feminine («Acquise avec…», «lecture mesurée avec…»); "fabricated"
  › «inventé»; "Stop does not fabricate completion" › «Arrêter ne simule pas une intégration terminée»; "navigates to" › «affiche»;
  "policy" › «règle» (FR3); "would wrap" › «sortirait de la plage représentable» (the review's wording); "fresh telescope view" › «vue du télescope à jour»;
  «la valeur E(B−V) du catalogue» (not «le E(B−V)»); "the Plot route" › «comme la trie Tracer (Plot)»; counted messages made
  count-neutral where the count can be 0 or 1 (`ph.coverage`, `ph.seriesPeriodCount`, `db.loaded`); «Tableau» › «tableau» in running
  text; «x87» joined with «ou» (not a slash).
The batches' own notes (uncertain «Tenir votre propre carnet» for "Keep your own record", «rappel» for callback, «poste» for bench,
«Abandonner» for Discard, «an» in log10(âge/an), «Rubriques connexes» for Related Topics, «Erreur de fichier d’aide») were read and
kept.

**Changed existing strings** (not add-only): FR1's `ph.domainLimit.Poisson-mean-bound` and `ph.domainLimit.all-duration-count-domain`
(«sur le {telescope}», above), and in review `ph.tiledLimit.Poisson-mean-bound` and `ph.tiledLimit.all-duration-count-domain`; the
glossary's `turn-?off` row (above). No shared core key was needed by both FR4 and FR5.

**Tests.**
- `src/i18n/fr/fr.test.ts`: the two new maps and variableStars complete (`ENGLISH_MAPS`, `COMPLETE_MAPS`), an FR4 coverage check (the
  649 keys of the nine prefixes), the fixer a no-op and the fr lint over 2,815 keys, 21 maps and the originals.
- `tests/i18n/languageText.ts`: `FRENCH_TEXT.done` gains `e04` and `e05`; `E04_E05_MAPS.fr`. So `e04.spec.ts` and `e05.spec.ts` run their
  checks in French under their own titles.
- `tests/i18n/walks/e04.ts` `SAME_IN.fr`: Minutes, Gain (e⁻/ADU), OK, Distance (pc). `walks/e05.ts` `SAME_IN.fr`: Minutes, OK,
  Confirmation, - Page 2, Distance (pc), Portrait, Type, Pixel (x, y), Max, Min / ampl. (each in `identical.json`).
- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: the E04 family (photometer session, Observational Results text and CSV, investigation) and the
  E05 family (investigation, cluster listing TXT and CSV, observation DAT, Recorded Results TXT and CSV, Cluster Data TXT and two HTL,
  variable-star TXT) gain `[fr]`: same names and SHA-256 as English, no French letter, « » or U+202F in any file, and the flow's `h1`
  is the French title. `FR_E04(key)` throws for a key French lacks (no English fallback before the release); `catalogCount` writes
  «31 147» for French too.
- `tests/i18n/areas.spec.ts`: the pinned French areas are unchanged (`classification`, `cluster`, `exposure`, `fits`, `lss`,
  `photometer`); `cluster` now has keys of its own, and the comment says so.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`; compared with main d4075a9 built the same way, chunk hashes normalised). fr: 10
chunks, 170.8 KB gzip (137.4 after FR3) — core 92.7 KB gzip (306.6 KB raw; 85.3 after FR3: `pleiadesLab`, `nc`, `pa`, `ownHr`,
`clusterLab`, the three maps), photometer 14.0 KB (1.8), cluster 8.6 KB (0.2; it has keys of its own now, so the build's keyless-chunk
warning of FR3 is gone), cluster+photometer 5.3 KB (new; Portuguese's three: 13.6, 8.3 and 5.2), classification 29.8, lss 9.1,
exposure 8.2, exposure+lss 1.4, fits 1.2, classification+cluster 0.4; pulsar and radio still empty. Spanish and Portuguese: every
chunk byte-identical, names included. English: every other file identical once chunk hashes are normalised, the entry and
index.html included. Deploy limits: 2,177 files.

**Verification** (2026-11-11, worktree `claude/front-fr4` from main d4075a9). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo`
and `src/components`: 1,106 tests in 44 files; the pins refreshed (only `dynamic-key-sites.json`: the fr variableStars map's
`printCurveLayout` head, like Spanish's and Portuguese's); `npm run build` with every check. Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu
fontconfig, two workers): the French `e04` and `e05` tests and the exports-identity `[fr]` variants, 24 (the E04 and E05 families
and FR0–FR3's); `e04`, `e05`, `observatory`, `shell` and `areas` in every language, 115 (one failure, the Portuguese E04 walk, passed
when rerun alone: a load flake); `npm run test:scripts` in its three CI parts, 107, 66 and 17; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/` 77.
The fr 320 px screenshots of the photometer, the cluster database and the variable-star page (`dist/` on loopback) were inspected.

**Independent review** (2026-11-11, Opus, high effort; head 4f6b07c). Every new and changed catalog string (587) and the three maps
were read against the English, with `originals.json` for the VIREO names (Consigner/Revoir, Résultats consignés, Séparé par des
virgules, Type sp., Limiter le déplacement, Magnitude de base, Attente du minuteur, Détecter le ciel, Définir E(B-V), Paramètres
d’âge et de distance de l’amas, Données d’amas CLEA…: one form each, as written there). Ten strings changed:
- *Same rule, two more strings, low:* FR1's `ph.tiledLimit.Poisson-mean-bound` and `ph.tiledLimit.all-duration-count-domain` still
  read «sur le télescope {telescope}» with the same `bench.name` («0.4m (16") Telescope»), now «sur le {telescope}» like
  `ph.domainLimit.*` (six strings in all).
- *Units, low:* "Myr" and "Gyr" are not French abbreviations (French writes «Ma», «Ga» or the words); as «années-lumière» is spelt
  out for "ly", `nc.preview` «{age} millions d’années», `nc.ageMyr` «(millions d’années)», `nc.limits` «d’environ 63 millions à 17.8
  milliards d’années», `db.limits` «17.8 milliards d’années» («log10(âge/an)» stays). Spanish and Portuguese keep Myr; unchanged.
- *Wording, low:* `clusterLab.step.trial.help` and `clusterLab.step.report.help` «un/le logarithme de l’âge» (was «log âge»);
  `obsHr.blocked` «(ou sa position en pixels sortirait de la plage représentable)» (the «ou si … sortait» clause did not attach to
  «ne peut pas être tracé»); `ph.domainNumbers` «5 000 rappels» with U+202F, as the English groups it.
- *Turn-off, kept:* the Paris Observatory's *Etymological Dictionary of Astronomy and Astrophysics* (dictionary.obspm.fr, entries
  "main-sequence turnoff" and "turnoff point", fetched 2026-11-11) gives «tournant final de la séquence principale», but no other
  French text found uses it; French Wikipedia *Séquence principale* names it «point de retournement» and Combes keeps «turn-off»;
  «point de coude» has no source. «point de retournement de la séquence principale» with «(turn-off)» at first mention stays.
- *Read and kept:* the `{cluster}` allowances (the lint's false-friend rule reads the placeholder's name; each of the six strings says
  «amas» and has no other «cluster»; setting placeholders aside in `outsideFrenchGlosses` belongs to the lint profile, which RT8/RT9
  edit, so it is left to a later front), `identical.json`'s 24 rows, `SAME_IN.fr` (exact strings, each in `identical.json` or FR1's
  identical originals), `FR_E04` and `frStart` (both throw for a missing key), the U+202F of the variableStars map's `grouped`.
- *Leakage, rechecked:* main d4075a9 and this branch built the same way; the 26 Spanish and Portuguese chunks byte-identical (names
  included); every non-French file identical after normalising chunk hashes; only `i18n-fr-*` differ, plus the new
  `cluster_photometer`; `dist/index.html` loads no French chunk and the registry line keeps `released:!1`.
- *Verification after the fixes:* `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo` and `src/components` 1,106; the pins
  refreshed with no change; `npm run build` with every check (fr core 92.7 KB gzip, 170.8 in all); Playwright `e04`, `e05`,

## French: the radio telescope (FR5)

The sixth French front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-fr.md` §6.2 "FR4 ∥ FR5" in the research root, with PT5 as its model;
it ran beside FR4 (E04 and E05). French stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=fr` reaches it, and fr and fr-* browsers still
open English. The translations are web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's; every file the site writes stays English. VIREO's
own radio captions (the control panel, Radio Simulation Options, Pulsar Parameters, the receiver windows, Identify Recorded Object,
the Save and unsaved-data questions, Cannot Close Receiver!, the drawn annotation's Sec and Deg) were among FR1's originals, so this
front adds web text only.

**What is translated.**
- **Catalog:** PT5's 646 keys, every key of `radio.*` (72), `radioRoute.*` (131), `radioAccess.*` (141), `radioReceiver.*` (200),
  `radioSimOptions.*` (20) and `pulsarParameters.*` (82); 645 were new (`radioRoute.nav` came with FR0), about 9,950 English words.
  Every radio file and prefix is at 100 % (`node scripts/i18n-coverage.mjs --lang fr`); `fr.json` now holds 2,875 keys
  (`fr.test.ts` pins the 646 as `FR5_KEYS`). `plr.*` and `pulsarLab.*` are E06's (FR6), as they were PT6's.
- **Maps, complete:** `radioAntenna` (324 strings), `radioR1` (11), `radioRecordSave` (17) and `radioFinishSave` (16)
  (`src/i18n/fr/maps/radio*.ts`), about 7,500 English words, written with single-quoted literals as the other fr maps (the dist
  check's markers read only those, so the `pulsar+radio` chunk is found by its text). `RADIO_MESSAGES_EN` stays catalog-backed.
- **Still English until FR6:** the receiver's Record Save dialog also renders texts of the `pulsar` map through
  `createPulsarTranslator` (`pulsar.rawExport.scope`, `.format`, `.inactive`, `.inactiveNone`, `.loss.*`, `pulsar.save.how`,
  `.extensionRule`, `.origin`, `.warning`, `.choose`, `.chooseAgain`, `.refusal.stale`), as PT5 waited for PT6. `radio.spec.ts`
  lists them as `TODO_FR6_PULSAR_KEYS` (PT5's mechanism: each an anchored pattern of its English text from `PulsarMessages.ts`),
  allowed only in fr and only on that step; FR6 removes the list. FR6's `pulsar.save.open` and `pulsar.rawExport.open` must read
  «Enregistrer la capture en .PLR (vérifiée)…» and «Télécharger la capture en .PLR…», which `radioReceiver.record.scope`,
  `.yesGated` and `radioSave.scope` quote, and `pulsar.save.choose` should be «Choisir», the word `radioSave.pendingNote` and the
  abandoned texts use for its Choose.
- **Canvas:** nothing new. `receiverScreenText` and `receiverMarkScreenText` translate only VIREO's pieces through `vireo()` (FR1's
  «Sec.», «Degrés», «Ouverture :», «Mètres», «Récepteur», «Aucun pulsar dans le faisceau !»).
- **Kept as recorded:** pulsar names, Pulsars.dat values, the stored ID “UNKNOWN”, «No Data», file names and hashes, and the
  constants the English writes ungrouped (the seed range 0 to 4294967295, the int32 bounds), as FR2 kept them.

**How it was translated.** Seven Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`), from the English, with the reviewed Spanish and Portuguese
beside each row as meaning context only, under FR1–FR3's brief (vous, infinitive controls, vous-imperative instructions, sentence
case, ordinary spaces before ; : ! ? and inside « » for the fixer, ’, the glossary and `originals.json` names with VIREO's English at
a first mention) extended with the radio terms below and the direction-button rule: four catalog batches (174 keys of `radio`,
`radioSimOptions` and `pulsarParameters`; 130 of `radioRoute`; 141 of `radioAccess`; 200 of `radioReceiver`) and three map
batches (the two halves of `radioAntenna`, 162 strings each; the three small maps, 44). Then the fixer's fix mode, then the strict
lint. The Opus implementer read every row against the English and changed 76 strings (7.5 %; 35 catalog, 41 map):
- **One form each:** stored (in memory) › «mémoriser» in the whole antenna map, as its second half wrote it (19 strings of the
  first half had «enregistrer», the word for Save); this browser version › «cette version pour navigateur» (7 had «du
  navigateur»); arcsec per tick › «secondes d’arc par cycle» (2 had «{rate}″»); UT, never «TU» (1); «Déclencher un cycle» for
  every Deliver … tick; recorded (a file's provenance) › «répertorié», as the batches chose it, where one string had «SHA-256
  enregistré»; Receiver Open › «ouverture du récepteur» (2 had «Récepteur ouvert»); readings › «lectures» (1 had «relevés»);
  the radio route › «la page du radiotélescope» (2 had «la page radio», 1 «télescopes radio»); Radio telescope control ›
  «Commande du radiotélescope» (the route title, now a glossary name; `radio.title` had «Contrôle»).
- **Meaning and wording:** a slew answered No is «Déplacement non confirmé» (was «refusé», as PT5's review); `manual-idle` «Aucun
  bouton de déplacement manuel n’est maintenu» (PT5's correction); «Si vous ajoutez un canal maintenant, Consigner est désarmé»
  (was «Ajouter un canal désarme maintenant Consigner»); «celles dont l’ascension droite» (coordinates are feminine); the
  altitude-limit message «la limite d’horizon (direction : {direction})» (the direction words are labels, «Nord», «Ouest»);
  `radioAccess.values.lastNotOk` «Pas OK»; «(construite par Mode)»; the UT format «AAAA-MM-JJTHH:MM:SS», as the date dialog's
  «AAAA/MM/JJ»; «Adaptation au navigateur» where the English says browser adaptation; `scenario.gates` «les commandes Ajouter
  l’objet actuel (Add Current Item) et Enregistrer (Save) de la liste d’objets»; «on ne prétend pas qu’Exécuter a été lancé».
- **Glosses the lint rejected or a glyph count:** a gloss never repeats a menu path's ▸ («Fichier ▸ Liste de pulsars...
  (Pulsar List)», 9 strings); a gloss is VIREO's English as the source writes it («(Set Date/Time ID)», «(Add Current)», «(Sound
  Sync)», «(Horz)», «(Identify)», «(Use B1950 Name)»); no `...` of a VIREO name mid-sentence where the English has none (3);
  ASCII brackets that read as glosses rewritten («(champ +0x408, TEnvironment1.Telescope1)», «(bouton Rechercher le suivant)»,
  «(section Configuration radio)», «Options numériques des Options de simulation radio»); nested glosses in [ ] («le menu
  Déplacer [Slew]»); elisions before a name («Comportement des boutons OK et Annuler», «la réponse Oui à la question de fin»).
- **The direction buttons** were N/S/E/O from the start (plan §3): every string that names the buttons or their directions says
  N/S/E/O («Maintenez appuyé N, S, E ou O», «État des boutons N/S/E/O», «les boutons Suivi (Tracking), E et O sont masqués»), and
  the button labels read «O (bouton d’origine O)» (the original button through `vireo()`; after a pole crossing «S (bouton d’origine N)»). `antenna.manual.keys` keeps the letter keys E, N, S and W, and names the button
  a keypad digit stands for: «6 (bouton O)»; the review added that the W key holds the O button («W pour le bouton O»).
- Term choices kept from the batches: recording (noun) › «acquisition», capture › «capture», Record › «Consigner» and to record ›
  «consigner» (FR1's originals); tick › «cycle» (FR2); the finish › «la procédure de fin», the finish question › «la question de
  fin»; mark › «repère» (Premier/Second repère); audition › «écoute»; reseed › «réinitialisation de la graine»; Drive ›
  «Entraînement»; host › «environnement hôte», host clock › «horloge de l’ordinateur»; flags › «indicateurs» (Delphi's Enabled
  kept as an identifier); built-in defaults › «valeurs par défaut intégrées» beside compiled defaults › «valeurs par défaut
  compilées»; Search from top / Find next › «Rechercher depuis le début» / «Rechercher le suivant»; Match case › «Respecter la
  casse» (FR1); Notice › «Avis»; captured startup route › «parcours de démarrage capturé»; Move up/down › «Monter»/«Descendre».

**Glossary** (`glossary.json`, add-only): two `terms` (arcsec › secondes d’arc, host clock › horloge de l’ordinateur; FR0's seed
already had the radio terms PT5 added, among them radio clock, press and hold, slew-timer, receiver monitor and audition) and
eight `names` (Receiver monitor › Moniteur du récepteur, Radio configuration › Configuration radio, pulsar exercise › TP sur les
pulsars, Manual marks › Repères manuels, Data properties › Propriétés des données, Trace audition › Écoute du tracé, Telescope
position › Position du télescope, Radio telescope control › Commande du radiotélescope). `identical.json` gains 16 rows (PT5's
reasons: placeholders, UT, the coordinate format, the dome byte 2, SHA-256; Longitude and OK, the same words in French; «Second»,
the second of two marks). No `lint-allowed.json` row was needed. **Shared entries changed:** none; every FR5 row is new.

**Tests.**
- `src/i18n/fr/fr.test.ts`: the four radio maps in `ENGLISH_MAPS` and `COMPLETE_MAPS`, an FR5 coverage check (`FR5_KEYS`, the 646
  keys of the six prefixes), the fixer a no-op and the fr lint over 2,875 keys, 23 maps and the originals (no errors).
- `tests/i18n/languageText.ts`: `FRENCH_TEXT.done` gains `radio`; `RADIO_MAPS.fr` (radioAntenna, radioRecordSave and fr
  `identical.json`). So `radio.spec.ts` runs its six language checks in French under their own titles (the walk against English,
  View original, the French originals, the canvas pieces, 320 and 1440 px).
- `tests/i18n/radio.spec.ts`: `TODO_FR6_PULSAR_KEYS` (above), and `englishSec`: the mark-label checks refused any "Sec", but
  FR1's French form of VIREO's Sec is «Sec.», which holds it; for a language whose form starts with "Sec" (French) only a bare
  "Sec" without the point is VIREO's English left over, and Spanish and Portuguese keep the plain substring check unchanged.
  `walks/radio.ts`: `SAME_IN.fr` (OK, Longitude, Latitude, Altitude, Pulsar, as pt-BR, and Options, Site..., Horizon, Type and
  Mode, French's identical originals, and the web's «Second») and `NOT_SAME_IN.fr` (No, no, Hrs and Bytes, which French translates; Radio, Radio... and E are the same words
  in French).
- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: the ES8a radio family (raw capture `.PLR`, VIREO's Save Recorded Measurements text and the
  investigation) gains `[fr]`: the same names and SHA-256 as English, no French letter, « » or U+202F in the files, and the route's
  French `h1` is checked.
- `tests/i18n/areas.spec.ts`: the pinned French areas are now all eight (`classification`, `cluster`, `exposure`, `fits`, `lss`,
  `photometer`, `pulsar`, `radio`): `radio` has its text and `pulsar` fetches the shared `pulsar+radio` module (the `radioR1` map,
  which Pulsar Analysis's File menu shows too), so no area is empty; the guard now expects none.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`; compared with main d4075a9 built the same way, chunk hashes normalised). fr: 12
chunks, 182.3 KB gzip after the review (182.2 before it; 137.4 KB on main) — radio 42.3 KB gzip (178.8 KB raw; Portuguese's is 40.4, Spanish's 40.3), pulsar+radio
1.3 KB (the `radioR1` map), pulsar 0.2 KB (a loader that only re-exports pulsar+radio, as pt-BR's after PT5), core 86.4 KB (285.2
KB raw; 85.3 on main: the radio keys the core holds, such as the route's title, scope and loading text and the data-property
labels). The radio budget is 60/90 KB, the sum 300/360. The dist check still warns that the fr `cluster` and `pulsar` chunks have
no marker text of their own (they only re-export shared modules) and finds them by name; FR4 and FR6 give them text, which the
release needs. `pulsar+radio` is found by its `radioR1` text. Spanish and Portuguese: all 13 chunks of each byte-identical, names
included. English: every other file identical once chunk hashes are normalised, the entry and `index.html` included (the fr core
imports no new module, so not even its preload list changes); the same-named `hubbleEngineTargets` pair only swaps its hash order.
Deploy limits: 2,179 files (2,176 on main).

**Verification** (2026-11-11, worktree `claude/front-fr5` from main d4075a9). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`,
`src/seo` and `src/components`: 1,106 tests in 44 files; the pins refreshed with no change; `npm run build` with every check.
Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig, two workers): `radio` and `areas` 40 (three French radio failures in the first
run, all fixed before the rerun: the French identical originals missing from `SAME_IN.fr`, and the «Sec.» check above), then
`radio` alone 19; `observatory`, `shell` and the exports-identity `[fr]` variants with every radio family variant 94; `npm run
test:scripts` in its three CI parts, 107, 66 and 17; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/` 77. No failures. The fr 320 px screenshots of
the walk (control panel, a placed mark, the Record Save dialog) were inspected.

**Independent review (FR5).** Every string this front adds was read against the English: the 646 catalog keys and the 368 strings
of the four maps. The French holds: vous throughout, infinitive buttons and menu items, vous-imperative instructions, FR1's VIREO
names (Consigner, Enregistrer, Localiser, Liste de pulsars..., Secondes horiz., Gain vert., Incr. de fréq., liste d’objets, Ajouter
l’objet actuel à la liste...) with VIREO's English at a first mention, the radio terms (radiotélescope, récepteur, faisceau, largeur
du faisceau, ouverture de l’antenne, temps sidéral, angle horaire, ascension droite, déclinaison, mesure de dispersion, densité
électronique, transit méridien, balayage, tracé), Save, Record and stored kept apart («Enregistrer», «Consigner», «mémoriser» in the
antenna map and «stocker» in the catalog; «enregistrer» only where a file or investigation is saved), and the typography. Three
strings changed:
1. *Wording (low; fixed).* `radioAccess.site.nonDefault` «Ces lectures ne sont pas certifiés» › «certifiées» (agreement);
   `radioAccess.numeric.optInHelp` «et enregistre la synchronisation du son» › «et stocke…» (VIREO stores the value in its options
   record; «enregistrer» is Save).
2. *The W key (low; fixed).* `antenna.manual.keys` listed the letter keys E, N, S and W but named a button only for the keypad digits
   («6 (bouton O)»), so a French reader of a panel whose buttons read N, S, E, O was not told which button W holds. It now says
   «la touche E, N, S ou W (W pour le bouton O ; …)». The code reads the typed letter W (`src/core/radioKeyboard.ts`), so this is
   the key the panel accepts.

**The direction buttons (accepted).** Their accessible names read «O (bouton d’origine O)»: `{original}` is VIREO's caption through
`vireo()`, as PT5's «L (botão original L)», so it names the button as the French panel shows it. For E and O the caption never
changes; the original letter only tells N and S apart after a pole crossing («S (bouton d’origine N)»). Showing VIREO's W there would
name a button the French panel does not have; the keyboard note above is where W belongs. Also accepted: the `TODO_FR6_PULSAR_KEYS`
exemption (16 exact keys, each an anchored pattern of its English text, only for fr and only on the Record Save step), `englishSec`
(only a bare "Sec" not followed by a point counts as English for a language whose form starts with "Sec"; Spanish and Portuguese keep
the substring check), `SAME_IN.fr` and `NOT_SAME_IN.fr` (exact strings, each with its identical.json or originals reason), and the
areas pin (all eight areas, none empty). Leakage, checked against main d4075a9 built the same way: the 26 es and pt-BR chunks
byte-identical with their names, the other 2,098 files identical once chunk hashes are normalised, `index.html` loads no fr chunk and
`languages.json` keeps fr unreleased. Verified after the last edit: `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo` and
`src/components` 1,106; the pins refreshed with no change; `npm run build` with every check; Playwright `radio`, `areas` and
`observatory` 69 and the exports-identity `[fr]` variants with every radio family variant 20, no failures.

**Left for later fronts.** FR6: the `pulsar` map (with it the Record Save dialog's pending texts and the removal of
`TODO_FR6_PULSAR_KEYS`), `plr.*` and `pulsarLab.*`, which also give the `pulsar` module marker text of its own before the release (the
dist check's warning under Sizes). FR-HELP: the radio help files behind the panel's Help menu and PLSLSHLP.

## French: E06 pulsars and E08 Object X (FR6)

The seventh French front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-fr.md` §6.2 "FR6 ∥ FR-HELP" in the research root, with PT6 as its
model; it ran beside FR-HELP (the French help files). French stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=fr` reaches it, and fr and
fr-* browsers still open English. The translations are web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's; every file the site writes stays
English, and E08's object stays hidden until the reveal in French too. VIREO's own captions on these screens (Review and its Find, the
comment editor, VIREO's Save and Clear questions, the print header, the camera's Information window, Spectral Range... and Help) were
among FR1's originals, so this front adds web text only.

**What is translated.**
- **Catalog:** the 134 keys left, PT6's scope: every key of `pulsarLab.*` (82 new; `pulsarLab.nav` came with FR0), `plr.*` (8) and
  `fits.*` (44), about 2,100 English words. `fr.json` now holds every en.json key (3,594): every file and prefix of `node
  scripts/i18n-coverage.mjs --lang fr` is at 100 % (`fr.test.ts` pins the 137 keys of the three prefixes as `FR6_KEYS`, and that no
  en.json key is missing). The merge script writes `fr.json` in en.json's order, so FR5's radio keys moved to their en.json place
  (no value changed).
- **Maps, complete:** `pulsar` (443 strings), `pulsarSavedRows` (83), `pulsarLoadSave` (25), `objectXLab` (completing FR0's two menu
  names), `objectXGuide`, `objectXMeasure` and `objectXSession` (`src/i18n/fr/maps/`), about 9,500 English words, single-quoted
  literals (the dist check's markers) except `pulsar.nativeSave.problem.reserved`, which lists the straight quote, as PT6 kept it. With
  them every French map but FR-HELP's `webHelp` and `helpViewer` is complete; `createPulsarTranslator` takes only a complete map, so
  the pulsar panel switched to French as a whole.
- **The verdict's article** follows the kind id, as in Spanish and Portuguese: «C’est une étoile.», «C’est une galaxie.», «C’est un
  quasar (QSO).», «C’est un pulsar.», «C’est un astéroïde.»; «Votre réponse est juste : c’est …» when the answer is right. The
  answer legend is «Nature de mon objet», so the bare kind names need no article.
- **Hidden identity:** no French string names or describes a target, and every string reads the same for every target (the neutral
  «Aucun spectre mesurable à cette position avec ce télescope.», the hidden asteroid count «astéroïdes de la liste actuelle inclus
  (le nombre s’affiche après la révélation)»); the kind words appear only as the five answer options (the same for every target) and in the verdict, which renders only
  inside `objectx-reveal`.
- **The receiver's Record Save dialog** (FR5) rendered 16 texts of the `pulsar` map in English; they are French now, and
  `TODO_FR6_PULSAR_KEYS` is gone from `tests/i18n/radio.spec.ts` (with its source reader and the `readFileSync` import). The buttons
  the radio strings quote read as FR5 asked: `pulsar.save.open` «Enregistrer la capture en .PLR (vérifiée)…» and
  `pulsar.rawExport.open` «Télécharger la capture en .PLR…» (quoted by `radioReceiver.record.scope`, `.yesGated` and
  `radioSave.scope`). `pulsar.save.choose` reads «Choisir un fichier et enregistrer…»: its Choose is «Choisir», the word
  `radioSave.pendingNote` and the abandoned texts name it by (as PT6's «Escolher arquivo e salvar…» is named «Escolher»); a bare
  «Choisir» would drop the English's "file and save", which the glossary's Save and file rows and the ellipsis require. A scan of
  every French catalog and map string that quotes a button in « » found each pulsar panel citation equal to that button's label.
- **Kept as recorded:** pulsar names, file names (.PLR, .CSV, ObjX-42-01.FIT, ObjX_MPC.CMO.sav…), the stored «No Data», VIREO's file
  label `Table Number:` (written in brackets, «(Table Number:)», so the fixer leaves its colon as the file has it), the Object X codes
  and ObjX labels, ObjectX.HTL's identifier and remark after the reveal, the asteroid designations and note codes, the recorded
  VizieR rows, FITS cards, hashes. JD stays JD, as in the rest of the French catalog.

**How it was translated.** Five Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`), from the English, with the reviewed Spanish and Portuguese
shown as meaning context only, under FR1–FR5's brief (vous, infinitive controls, vous-imperative instructions, sentence case,
ordinary spaces before ; : ! ? and inside « » for the fixer, ’, the glossary and `originals.json` names with VIREO's English at a first
mention, FR5's Save/Record/store split and radio terms) extended with this front's terms, the three fixed button wordings and the
hidden-identity rule: the catalog (134 keys, merged with `scripts/i18n-batch.mjs merge --lang fr`), the `pulsar` map in two halves
(225 and 218 strings), the two small pulsar maps (108) and the four Object X maps (written from Portuguese's TypeScript shape). Then
the fixer's fix mode, then the strict lint. The Opus implementer read every row (901 strings) against the English and changed 36
(4.0 %; 10 catalog, 26 map):
- **Glosses the lint rejected:** a gloss is VIREO's English as the source writes it, never a French text in ASCII brackets and never
  `...` where the English has `…` («Localiser un pulsar… (Find Pulsar)», «Définir les coordonnées… (Set Coordinates)», «(Identify)»
  for the receiver's Identify, «[Save recorded measurements]» nested in a parenthesis, «La commande Localiser (Find)»); French
  bracket text starts lower case or is rewritten («(réglage Gain vert.)», «(soit CR LF, soit LF)», «(dix avec Maj)», «(valeur 128 :
  potentiellement dangereux)», «Δ (ua)», the SI French symbol of the astronomical unit, «une marque d’ordre des octets, ou BOM»);
  «l’ATNF Pulsar Catalogue (Manchester et al. 2005)» without a French semicolon inside the citation.
- **The glossary:** press › «appuyer» (two load-save strings had «cliquez sur» for "press"); blink › «comparaison par
  clignotement» (the guide's step title had «Comparer et mesurer»); astrometry › «au programme externe d’astrométrie CLEA Astrometry
  Toolkit»; "will not help" › «ne vous aidera pas».
- **Meaning and wording:** «Enregistrer et Annuler fonctionnent de nouveau» (the English names Save and Cancel; the batch had
  «Choisir»); «quand vous avez appuyé sur Enregistrer» (was «lancé l’enregistrement»); «Réessayez une fois l’opération terminée»
  (two strings had «une fois terminée» with nothing to agree with); `pulsarLab.armed` «si vous ajoutez un canal maintenant, il sera
  désarmé», as FR5 corrected the same English; «« Démarrer » laisse Suivi désactivé»; «avec le télescope de 0.4 m» (`{telescope}`
  is an aperture); «Prochaine marque :» for the measuring tool's legend; «la boîte de dialogue s’ouvre» (was «le dialogue»);
  `plr.savedRows.help` quotes the button «Utiliser ce fichier», as the viewer's own messages do.
- Term choices kept from the batches: recording (the data) › «acquisition», capture › «capture», Record › «Consigner», recorded ›
  «consigné», stored › «mémorisé» (FR5); Review › «Revoir (Review)»; Append › «Ajouter» while the web button reads «Ajouter les
  mesures consignées à un fichier de résultats enregistré (.csv)…»; Export › «Exporter»; Dismiss › «Fermer»; Discard › «Écarter»;
  viewer › «visionneuse»; native writer › «routine d’écriture native» (`pulsar`) and «module d’écriture natif» (`pulsarSavedRows`);
  headerless › «sans en-tête»; line endings › «fins de ligne»; carriage return / line feed › «retour chariot» / «saut de ligne»;
  placeholder › «texte de remplacement»; display factor › «facteur d’affichage»; pan › «défiler» / «Pas de défilement» (the review's change); zoom in/out ›
  «Zoom avant/arrière»; low/high cut › «Seuil bas/haut»; trail › «traînée» (the camera's word); field box › «cadre du champ»; mark ›
  «marque»; near-Earth › «géocroiseur»; reveal › «Révéler l’identité» / «la révélation»; the export's Table number › «Numéro de
  table de laboratoire»; hash › «empreinte»; Subnormal › «dénormalisé»; Classify (the route) › «Classification stellaire»; the
  observatory names in the guide are FR1's («Saisir les coordonnées», «Pointer vers les coordonnées», «Champ de vue › Télescope ·
  15′», «Déplacer vers l’objet sélectionné») and the radio ones FR5's («Démarrer le TP sur les pulsars», «Réessayer le catalogue
  inclus», «Scénario de l’horloge radio»).

**FR4's two follow-ups.**
1. `galaxyExposure.telescopeUnsupported` (FR2) said «sur le télescope {telescope}» although `{telescope}` is VIREO's name, which already
   ends in "Telescope"; it now says «sur le {telescope}», as FR4's `ph.*Limit.*` do.
2. The fr profile's `outsideFrenchGlosses` (`src/i18n/lint/profiles.ts`) now also skips a `{placeholder}` token, and the fr profile
   sets `forbiddenSetAside` to the same tokens, so the glossary's forbidden «cluster» no longer matches the placeholder `{cluster}`:
   the six word-level `cluster` allowances of `lint-allowed.json` (`db.loaded`, `db.catalogDraft`, `db.catalogConfirmed`,
   `db.chartDescription`, `db.prefilled`, `clusterLab.dataset`) are gone. Two fixtures (`src/i18n/lint/fixtures.ts`, typography and
   glossary) prove that «{cluster} : données de l’amas chargées.» passes and that a real «cluster» beside the placeholder («{cluster} :
   données du cluster chargées.») is still caught by both rules. Only French's profile changed.

**Shared files (add-only).** `identical.json` gains 44 rows (PT6's reasons in French terms: the seven empty statuses, SHA-256, the
gain field names, Zoom, Gain, Points, Horizontal, Vertical (s), Image, Solution, #, the dash, CR LF and LF, OK, the placeholder-only
formats, the telescope apertures, the date placeholder, the list's band letter and note numbers, x, y, UT, «quasar (QSO)» and
«pulsar»); `lint-allowed.json` gains three (`pulsar.nativeSave.problem.reserved`'s straight quote and `objectXLab.imageSkipped`'s
sample, PT6's reasons, and `pulsar.nativeSave.filter` «Type de fichier : {filter}», the Windows French label of the Save dialog's
type list, which the glossary's Save row would otherwise demand) and loses the six `cluster` rows above. No glossary row was added or
changed. **Shared entries changed:** `galaxyExposure.telescopeUnsupported` (follow-up 1) and the six removed allowances; no other
existing entry of a shared file changed.

(unchanged). The plan's risk of a core above 115 KB did not happen. The dist check prints no warning: the fr `pulsar` chunk now has

Spanish and Portuguese: all 26 chunks byte-identical, names included. English and everything else: the same 2,180 files, identical
once chunk hashes are normalised; only the four fr chunks this front fills (core, fits, pulsar, pulsar+radio) differ. Deploy limits:

**Independent review (FR6).** Every string this front adds was read against the English: the 134 catalog keys and the 901 strings
of the seven maps. The French holds: vous throughout, infinitive buttons and menu items, vous-imperative instructions, FR1's VIREO
names (Consigner, Revoir, Localiser, Enregistrer, Paramètres du pulsar, Définir les coordonnées..., Ajouter un canal, Fréq. MHz, Gain
vert., Récepteur, Respecter la casse) with VIREO's English at a first mention, FR5's Save/Record/stored split, the pulsar terms
(période, mesure de dispersion, retard de dispersion, impulsion, chronométrage, rapport signal sur bruit) and the Object X ones
(comparaison par clignotement, solution astrométrique, constantes de plaque, géocroiseur, traînée), and every observatory, radio and
toolbar name the guides quote equals that control's French label (`sky.coordinates`, `sky.pointCoordinates`, `sky.view` ›
`sky.telescope`, `motion.selected`, `project.save`, `radioRoute.exercise.start`, `radioRoute.exercise.catalogRetry`,
`classImport.input`). Nineteen strings changed:
1. *An invented label (low; fixed).* `pulsarLab.step.period.formula` sent the reader to a line «« Période à la date de la capture »»
   of Pulsar Parameters, which has no such label: the English names the Period field at the capture date. It now says «champ
   Période à la date de la capture».
2. *One form (low; fixed).* "the recorded original" (a file's provenance) is FR5's «répertorié» (`pulsarParameters.*`); three
   `pulsarLab` strings had «référencé» (`step.catalog.help`, `data.supplied.help`, `recover.catalog`). The double quote is «guillemet
   double» in the `pulsar` map; two `pulsarSavedRows` rejections had «guillemet droit». "read back equal" is «relus à l’identique» in
   every string; `pulsar.save.verified` and `pulsar.save.lastCopyNote` had «relus identiques».
3. *Grammar (low; fixed).* The four "not available now: it needs X, and no other dialog…" texts (`pulsar.rawExport.unavailable`,
   `pulsar.save.unavailable`, `pulsar.export.unavailable`, `pulsar.append.unavailable`) read «il faut X, et aucune autre boîte de
   dialogue…», a negative without «ne»; they now read «il faut X, sans autre boîte de dialogue ouverte, lecture de fichier, acquisition
   du récepteur en cours ni donnée en attente».
4. *Pan is not Slew (low; fixed).* The glossary keeps «Déplacer» for VIREO's Slew (telescope motion); the pulsar display's pan
   controls, which scroll the time window, said «Déplacer à gauche/droite» and «Pas de déplacement», and FR-HELP's help says
   «Défilement horizontal». They now read «Défiler vers la gauche», «Défiler vers la droite», «Pas de défilement (échantillons par
   pas)», with `pulsar.toolbar.panNote` («bouton de défilement», «défiler d’un pas»), `pulsar.outcome.pan-rate` («Pas de défilement
   défini.»), `pulsar.outcome.panned` («Fenêtre décalée.») and `pulsar.guide.zoom` («Le zoom et le défilement») to match. The radio
   antenna's manual slew buttons keep «déplacement», which is telescope motion.
Screen names changed for the help: the pan buttons and the pan-rate field above. No Object X window title changed («La quête de
l’Objet X», «Mesurer les positions», «Guide du TP E08» stay).

**E08's hidden identity (accepted).** Before the reveal no French string of the four Object X maps names or implies a target: the kind
words appear in `scope` and `spectrumHelp` (the same for every target, as in English), in the five answer options and in the verdict,
whose article follows the kind id and which renders only after the reveal; the neutral unavailable spectrum, the hidden asteroid count
and the masked names read the same for every target, and no adjective or article agrees with the target's kind (every reference is
«l’objet», «votre objet»). The walk's verdict pattern catches both French verdicts («C’est une/un», «Votre réponse est juste»).

**The lint change (accepted).** `outsideFrenchGlosses` (fr only) now also skips a match that starts inside or ends inside a
`{placeholder}` token (a look-behind for an open `{name` and a look-ahead for `name}`), and `forbiddenSetAside` (the fr profile only;
Spanish keeps its own pulsar-name set-aside, other profiles none) blanks `{name}` tokens before the glossary's forbidden words. Both
match only `{` + letters, digits or `_` + `}`, which is never visible text, so a word outside a placeholder is still checked; the two
fixtures prove a real «cluster» beside `{cluster}` fails both rules. `src/i18n/lint/profiles.ts` changed nowhere else.

## French: help (FR-HELP)

The French help front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-fr.md` §4.6 and §6.2 "FR6 ∥ FR-HELP" in the research root, with ES-HELP
and PT-HELP as its models. French stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=fr` reaches it. VIREO's 38 bundled help files and
OpenVireo's own help are translated as web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's: the originals under `public/original`, their
bytes, SHA-256 and provenance are unchanged, and «Voir l’original (anglais)» shows VIREO's exact text.

**What is translated.**
- **The 38 help modules** `src/i18n/fr/help/<NAME>.ts` (`{language:'fr',sourceName,sourceSha256,text}`) in VIREO's own file format,
  as ES-HELP and PT-HELP describe it: LF, every TXT line at 72 columns or fewer, titles at 65 or fewer, the same LST rows, kinds and
  file tokens (the unterminated `'Classer les spectres' 'begin` and `'Types d’étoiles variables' 'begin` rows reproduced; HUBHLP and
  LSSHLP keep their blank lines and stay refused; MLTSPHLP and SPLSTHLP stay empty; OBJXHLP and TOOLSHLP, byte-identical originals,
  have identical translations), each section header the row title it opens, SPECLNS.LNL equal to the original and SPECLNS.TXT's line
  headers VIREO's. About 18,600 English words, 22,800 French.
- **Characters** (plan §4.6). Latin-1 holds « », æ and every accented French letter, not ’, œ, Œ or U+202F; the help sets became
  Unicode text in SC7, so French writes them as characters, as the screen does: ’ for the apostrophe (inside LST titles too, where a
  straight `'` would end VIREO's quoted token), œ, U+202F before ; : ! ? and inside « », and between thousands («2 400 000»). Quotes are
  « » (no straight `"` in a TXT file, a test). `LATIN1_HELP.fr` is `'\u202f\u2019\u0153\u0152'` (U+202F ’ œ Œ); the check that the text parsers read
  a Latin-1 set as the byte parsers read its bytes maps each added character one for one onto a Latin-1 character no French help
  holds (U+202F → U+00A0, as Portuguese; ’ → ´; œ Œ → ø Ø; a test forbids ´ ø Ø in the French text). Departure from the plan: it
  wrote ’→' and œ→oe there, but a `'` inside a title ends the LST token in the byte parser and oe lengthens a full line by a column.
  The test also maps each title it seeks (the byte parser's seek reads the mapped text).
- **The related-topics marker** «Rubriques connexes :» (`help:{related:'RUBRIQUES CONNEXES'}` in `src/i18n/languages.json`); VIREO's
  "See also:" reads «Voir aussi :»; VIREO's NOTE is «REMARQUE» everywhere (PT-HELP's lesson), followed by a lower-case word.
- **Maps, complete:** `helpViewer` («Traduction : adaptation web d’OpenVireo (ce n’est pas un texte du CLEA)» · «Voir l’original
  (anglais)» · «Voir la traduction», the fold note below, the Spectral Line Table window) and `webHelp` (Aide › Rubriques d’aide…,
  with the plan's sentence on numbers in the second paragraph of «Ce qu’est OpenVireo», as Portuguese placed its own: «À l’écran, les
  nombres utilisent le point décimal et une espace fine entre les milliers (par exemple 1.5 et 24 000) ; saisissez aussi les
  décimales avec un point. Les fichiers enregistrés par le site restent en anglais, comme VIREO les écrit.»). Two `identical.json`
  rows: `map:helpViewer.spectralLines.ok` (OK) and the topic title «Instruments».
- **The search fold** (`foldHelpText`, `src/core/cleaHelp.ts`): ’ reads as ', œ and æ as oe and ae, U+202F and U+00A0 as a space,
  so a typed «l'etoile» finds «L’étoile», «noeud» finds «nœud», and «2 400 000» or «Prêt ?» typed with ordinary spaces or a French
  keyboard's no-break space finds the help's. æ and U+00A0 are the Latin-1 characters whose fold changes (no Spanish or Portuguese
  help row holds either; `cleaHelp.test.ts` names them), and U+202F now reads as a space in the Portuguese help too. The fold maps
  each character the same way in the query and the rows, so every earlier match still matches: Spanish and Portuguese only find
  more (a typed space finds Portuguese «2 400 000»), never less. "Match case" still compares exactly. The `helpViewer` map's fold note says
  all of this.

**How it was translated.** Six Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`), from the English, with the reviewed Spanish and Portuguese
help as meaning context and models of the format, under one brief (the plan's §3–§5: vous and its imperatives, infinitive controls,
sentence case, accented capitals, the IAU OAE terms, «raie» for a spectral line, «décalage vers le rouge» in full, «point de
retournement de la séquence principale (turn-off)», VIREO names from `originals.json` and the glossary in « » with VIREO's English in
brackets at the first mention in each section; ordinary spaces before ; : ! ? and inside « » for the fixer, ’ typed, never a line
starting with a mark that belongs to the previous one): VIRMNHLP with the two reminders; PULSRHLP with PLSLSHLP and PULSRLST;
PLSANHLP, PULSRANL, SPECHLP and SPECLNS; PHOTOHLP, OBSRVHLP and CAMHLP (coupled through ObsrvHlp.txt); CLASSHLP and MEASHLP;
HRANALHLP, VARTYPEHLP, WDGPLTHLP and the eight index-only files. Then the fixer's fix mode (it rewrites `help/*.ts` too), then the
strict checks. The Opus implementer wrote the two maps (every quoted label looked up in the fr catalog, maps and originals) and read
every paragraph of the 38 modules against the English, changing 120 lines in 16 modules beyond the fixer's spacing:
- **One name per thing:** the index rows shared by VIRMNHLP and OBXMNHLP read the same (Choisir un télescope, Terminer l’exercice,
  Accès au clavier, which OBSRVHLP, CLASSHLP and MEASHLP also cite); VIREO's tools are «Outil de mesure des spectres» and «Outil
  d’analyse de pulsars radio» everywhere (as `fr.json`'s «Analyse de pulsars»; the batches wrote «de spectre(s)» and «des pulsars»),
  and its Exit item «Quitter la mesure des spectres»; VIREO's switches are «interrupteurs» (the dome's Open/Close and the Fine/Coarse
  switch were «commutateur», while the slider switches and the screen say «interrupteur»); MouseKeys is «Touches souris (MouseKeys)»
  in OBSRVHLP as in the other files; the classification help's standards are «étalons» as E03's screen (spectres étalons, la liste
  des étalons, the «Étalons» (Standards) box, the title «Charger des spectres étalons»); its display options use E03's names («Photo
  en niveaux de gris», «Inverser les niveaux de gris photographiques»); the measuring tool's readouts are «affichages» (the glossary's
  readout, not «lecteurs»); the redshift letter is z (VIREO's «(Z)» and «« Z »», as FR1's originals); LSSHLP's wedge row reads
  «Accéder au programme de diagramme en cône» (the batch had guillemets around «cône»).
- **Lint findings fixed in the text** (the new French help lint below): brackets that read as English glosses but held French
  («(Std - Inc.)», «(Windows 95 seulement)», «(N, S, E, O)», «(400, 600 et 1400 MHz)», «(S/B)», «(File->Data->Record/Review)»), a gloss
  the English does not hold («(Grayscale Photo)», VIREO writes `Grayscale "Photo"`, dropped), «le ou les» rewritten twice, glosses
  equal to the French removed («(Options...)», «(Isochrones...)», «(Gain)»).
- **French:** «Vous trouverez peut-être plus précis de mesurer les raies une à une» (was «Vous pouvez estimer plus précis…»), the
  elisions «de l’interrupteur», «L’interrupteur» after the switch rename, a lower-case word after «REMARQUE :» and «ATTENTION :» (8).
- **Kept from the batches:** «Se connecter», «Exécuter l’exercice» and «Touches souris», as the helpMenu map; «Interrupteurs à
  curseur» for VIREO's Slider Switches (the glossary's slider is «curseur»; the mouse pointer is «pointeur»); «acquisition» for a
  radio recording with «Consigner» (Record) for its button; «instrument de passage au méridien»; VIREO's two spaces after a full stop
  where the original has them; VIREO's own quirks (the numbering 1, 2, 4 in «Charger les données enregistrées», whitespace-only lines,
  the topic names a «Rubriques connexes» line cites without an index row).
